        Idea:
Two goals for the SWE-bench eval harness: (1) Scale to 10 parallel workers mid-run without disrupting existing work. Currently 5 workers each have 100 tasks assigned at launch via contiguous chunking. Need to support adding more workers that pick up UNCLAIMED tasks — tasks not yet started by any existing worker. This means tracking claimed tasks in a shared manifest file (not just per-worker configs). The new workers should be launchable as a separate parallel.py invocation that reads the manifest to avoid overlap. (2) Support running multiple model configs simultaneously on the same 500 tasks. E.g. run Qwen+GLM alongside Claude+GPT on SWE-bench Verified, each with their own results dir and workers, sharing the same task list but with separate predictions dirs. This means the run_name should encode the model config (e.g. swebench-verified-qwen-glm vs swebench-verified-opus-gpt). Current state: evals/parallel.py splits tasks into N chunks at launch, writes temp worker configs, spawns subprocesses. Results go to results/{run_name}/worker-{i}/. Predictions go to workspaces-{i}/_swebench_predictions/. Key constraint: must not disrupt the current 5-worker run (PID 56575, generating patches for 500 tasks). The manifest approach should be backwards-compatible — if no manifest exists, fall back to current chunking.

  "meta_commentary": "**Execution guidance:**\n\n**Parallelism**: T1, T2, and T8 have no dependencies and can be implemented concurrently. T3 and T4 can start as soon as T2 and T1 finish respectively. T5 and T6 are the core integration tasks and depend on both T1 and T2. T7 depends on T5+T6. T9 depends on T8. T10 is the final validation gate.\n\n**Critical path**: T1 \u2192 T5/T6 \u2192 T7 \u2192 T10. The manifest module (T2) is also on the critical path since T5 and T6 both need it.\n\n**Biggest risk**: The orchestrator manifest-wait loop (T6 step 5). The plan leaves the timeout question open \u2014 implement a 4-hour default with a configurable override. On timeout, log which task IDs are stranded in 'claimed' state and proceed to combine/score with whatever is done. Do NOT hang forever.\n\n**Second biggest risk**: The --join config validation (T7). The plan acknowledges this is unresolved. Implement it by saving _run_config.json at run start and having --join compare key fields (benchmark, models, swebench_dataset) against it. Warn loudly on mismatch, but allow override with a --force flag for advanced users.\n\n**Prediction path fix context**: The bug at swe_bench.py:247 is real \u2014 workers write to `workspace_path.parent/_swebench_predictions` while parallel.py:38 reads from `results_root/_swebench_predictions`. These are different directories. The fix in T4 uses config.predictions_dir (set by parallel.py to the results_root path) to align them. In legacy (non-parallel) mode, the old path is preserved.\n\n**EvalConfig slots=True**: This dataclass uses `slots=True`, which means unknown kwargs to EvalConfig() raise TypeError. This is why T1 must add the new fields to the class \u2014 you can't just pass them through as extra kwargs. But the defaults mean old configs without these fields work fine.\n\n**Don't touch PID 56575**: The running 5-worker job has already-written temp config files on disk. Those files don't have the new manifest fields, but since the new fields have defaults, load_config() will construct EvalConfig without them \u2192 manifest_path='' \u2192 legacy mode. No disruption.\n\n**Testing strategy**: T3 validates the manifest in isolation. T10 validates the full integration but needs stubs \u2014 the actual eval loop calls megaplan/hermes which need API keys. Mock at the benchmark.score() boundary to just write a prediction file and return a ScoringResult."

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

 \"\"\"JSON-backed task manifest coordinated with a sidecar flock lock.\"\"\"\n+\n+    def __init__(self, path: Path, data: dict[str, Any]):\n+        self.path = Path(path)\n+        self.lock_path = self.path.parent / f\"{self.path.name}.lock\"\n+        self._data = data\n+\n+    @classmethod\n+    def create(cls, path: str | Path, tasks: list[str]) -> \"TaskManifest\":\n+        manifest = cls(Path(path), {})\n+        payload = manifest._build_payload(tasks)\n+        with manifest._lock():\n+            manifest._write_unlocked(payload)\n+        manifest._data = payload\n+        return manifest\n+\n+    @classmethod\n+    def load(cls, path: str | Path) -> \"TaskManifest\":\n+        manifest = cls(Path(path), {})\n+        manifest._data = manifest._read_current()\n+        return manifest\n+\n+    @classmethod\n+    def load_or_create(cls, path: str | Path, tasks: list[str]) -> \"TaskManifest\":\n+        manifest = cls(Path(path), {})\n+        with manifest._lock():\n+            if manifest.path.exists():\n+                payload = manifest._read_unlocked()\n+            else:\n+                payload = manifest._build_payload(tasks)\n+                manifest._write_unlocked(payload)\n+        manifest._data = payload\n+        return manifest\n+\n+    def claim_batch(self, worker_id: str, batch_size: int) -> list[str]:\n+        \"\"\"Atomically claim up to ``batch_size`` pending tasks for a worker.\"\"\"\n+        if batch_size < 1:\n+            return []\n+\n+        claimed_at = _utc_now()\n+        with self._lock():\n+            payload = self._read_unlocked()\n+            claimed: list[str] = []\n+            for instance_id, task in payload[\"tasks\"].items():\n+                if task.get(\"status\") != \"pending\":\n+                    continue\n+                task[\"status\"] = \"claimed\"\n+                task[\"worker\"] = worker_id\n+                task[\"claimed_at\"] = claimed_at\n+                claimed.append(instance_id)\n+                if len(claimed) >= batch_size:\n+                    break\n+            if claimed:\n+                self._write_unlocked(payload)\n+            self._data = payload\n+            return claimed\n+\n+    def mark_done(self, instance_id: str, worker_id: str) -> None:\n+        done_at = _utc_now()\n+        with self._lock():\n+            payload = self._read_unlocked()\n+            task = _require_task(payload, instance_id)\n+            task[\"status\"] = \"done\"\n+            task[\"worker\"] = worker_id\n+            task[\"done_at\"] = done_at\n+            self._write_unlocked(payload)\n+            self._data = payload\n+\n+    def mark_error(self, instance_id: str, worker_id: str, error: str) -> None:\n+        done_at = _utc_now()\n+        with self._lock():\n+            payload = self._read_unlocked()\n+            task = _require_task(payload, instance_id)\n+            task[\"status\"] = \"error\"\n+            task[\"worker\"] = worker_id\n+            task[\"error\"] = error\n+            task[\"done_at\"] = done_at\n+            self._write_unlocked(payload)\n+            self._data = payload\n+\n+    def unclaimed_count(self) -> int:\n+        payload = self._read_current()\n+        self._data = payload\n+        return sum(1 for task in payload[\"tasks\"].values() if task.get(\"status\") == \"pending\")\n+\n+    def all_done(self) -> bool:\n+        payload = self._read_current()\n+        self._data = payload\n+        return not any(task.get(\"status\") in {\"pending\", \"claimed\"} for task in payload[\"tasks\"].values())\n+\n+    def done_task_ids(self) -> set[str]:\n+        payload = self._read_current()\n+        self._data = payload\n+        return {\n+            instance_id\n+            for instance_id, task in payload[\"tasks\"].items()\n+            if task.get(\"status\") == \"done\"\n+        }\n+\n+    def summary(self) -> dict[str, int]:\n+        payload = self._read_current()\n+        self._data = payload\n+        counts = {\n+            \"total_tasks\": int(payload.get(\"total_tasks\", len(payload.get(\"tasks\", {})))),\n+            \"pending\": 0,\n+            \"claimed\": 0,\n+            \"done\": 0,\n+        }\n+        for task in payload[\"tasks\"].values():\n+            status = str(task.get(\"status\", \"pending\"))\n+            counts[status] = counts.get(status, 0) + 1\n+        return counts\n+\n+    def next_worker_id(self) -> str:\n+        payload = self._read_current()\n+        self._data = payload\n+        seen: set[int] = set()\n+        for task in payload[\"tasks\"].values():\n+            worker = task.get(\"worker\")\n+            if not isinstance(worker, str) or not worker.startswith(\"worker-\"):\n+                continue\n+            suffix = worker.removeprefix(\"worker-\")\n+            if suffix.isdigit():\n+                seen.add(int(suffix))\n+        return f\"worker-{max(seen, default=-1) + 1}\"\n+\n+    def _build_payload(self, tasks: list[str]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+        ordered_tasks: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {}\n+        for instance_id in tasks:\n+            ordered_tasks.setdefault(instance_id, {\"status\": \"pending\"})\n+        return {\n+            \"version\": 1,\n+            \"created_at\": _utc_now(),\n+            \"total_tasks\": len(ordered_tasks),\n+            \"tasks\": ordered_tasks,\n+        }\n+\n+    def _read_current(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+        with self._lock():\n+            return self._read_unlocked()\n+\n+    def _read_unlocked(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+        if not self.path.exists():\n+            raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Manifest not found: {self.path}\")\n+        data = json.loads(self.path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n+        if not isinstance(data, dict):\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Manifest at {self.path} must contain a JSON object\")\n+        tasks = data.get(\"tasks\")\n+        if not isinstance(tasks, dict):\n+            raise ValueError(f\"Manifest at {self.path} is missing a tasks map\")\n+        return data\n+\n+    def _write_unlocked(self, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n+        self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+        tmp_path = self.path.parent / f\"{self.path.name}.{os.getpid()}.tmp\"\n+        tmp_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=False), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+        tmp_path.replace(self.path)\n+\n+    def _lock(self) -> _ManifestLock:\n+        return _ManifestLock(self.lock_path)\n+\n+\n+class _ManifestLock:\n+    def __init__(self, path: Path):\n+        self.path = path\n+        self._handle = None\n+\n+    def __enter__(self) -> \"_ManifestLock\":\n+        self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+        self._handle = self.path.open(\"a+\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+        fcntl.flock(self._handle.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n+        return self\n+\n+    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n+        assert self._handle is not None\n+        fcntl.flock(self._handle.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_UN)\n+        self._handle.close()\n+        self._handle = None\n+\n+\n+def _require_task(payload: dict[str, Any], instance_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+    task = payload.get(\"tasks\", {}).get(instance_id)\n+    if not isinstance(task, dict):\n+        raise KeyError(f\"Unknown manifest task: {instance_id}\")\n+    return task\n+\n+\n+def _utc_now() -> str:\n+    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()\n*** End Patch\n"

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Two goals for the SWE-bench eval harness: (1) Scale to 10 parallel workers mid-run without disrupting existing work. Currently 5 workers each have 100 tasks assigned at launch via contiguous chunking. Need to support adding more workers that pick up UNCLAIMED tasks — tasks not yet started by any existing worker. This means tracking claimed tasks in a shared manifest file (not just per-worker configs). The new workers should be launchable as a separate parallel.py invocation that reads the manifest to avoid overlap. (2) Support running multiple model configs simultaneously on the same 500 tasks. E.g. run Qwen+GLM alongside Claude+GPT on SWE-bench Verified, each with their own results dir and workers, sharing the same task list but with separate predictions dirs. This means the run_name should encode the model config (e.g. swebench-verified-qwen-glm vs swebench-verified-opus-gpt). Current state: evals/parallel.py splits tasks into N chunks at launch, writes temp worker configs, spawns subprocesses. Results go to results/{run_name}/worker-{i}/. Predictions go to workspaces-{i}/_swebench_predictions/. Key constraint: must not disrupt the current 5-worker run (PID 56575, generating patches for 500 tasks). The manifest approach should be backwards-compatible — if no manifest exists, fall back to current chunking.

  "meta_commentary": "**Execution guidance:**\n\n**Parallelism**: T1, T2, and T8 have no dependencies and can be implemented concurrently. T10 is the final validation gate.\n\n**Critical path**: T1 \u2192 T5/T6 \u2192 T7 \u2192 T10. The manifest module (T2) is also on the critical path since T5 and T6 both need it.\n\n**Biggest risk**: The orchestrator manifest-wait loop (T6 step 5). The plan leaves the timeout question open \u2014 implement a 4-hour default with a configurable override. On timeout, log which task IDs are stranded in 'claimed' state and proceed to combine/score with whatever is done. Do NOT hang forever.\n\n**Second biggest risk**: The --join config validation (T7). The plan acknowledges this is unresolved. Implement it by saving _run_config.json at run start and having --join compare key fields (benchmark, models, swebench_dataset) against it. Warn loudly on mismatch, but allow override with a --force flag for advanced users.\n\n**Prediction path fix context**: The bug at swe_bench.py:247 is real \u2014 workers write to `workspace_path.parent/_swebench_predictions` while parallel.py:38 reads from `results_root/_swebench_predictions`. These are different directories. The fix in T4 uses config.predictions_dir (set by parallel.py to the results_root path) to align them. In legacy (non-parallel) mode, the old path is preserved.\n\n**EvalConfig slots=True**: This dataclass uses `slots=True`, which means unknown kwargs to EvalConfig() raise TypeError. This is why T1 must add the new fields to the class \u2014 you can't just pass them through as extra kwargs. But the defaults mean old configs without these fields work fine.\n\n**Don't touch PID 56575**: The running 5-worker job has already-written temp config files on disk. Those files don't have the new manifest fields, but since the new fields have defaults, load_config() will construct EvalConfig without them \u2192 manifest_path='' \u2192 legacy mode. No disruption.\n\n**Testing strategy**: T3 validates the manifest in isolation. T10 validates the full integration but needs stubs \u2014 the actual eval loop calls megaplan/hermes which need API keys. Mock at the benchmark.score() boundary to just write a prediction file and return a ScoringResult."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Two goals for the SWE-bench eval harness: (1) Scale to 10 parallel workers mid-run without disrupting existing work. Currently 5 workers each have 100 tasks assigned at launch via contiguous chunking. Need to support adding more workers that pick up UNCLAIMED tasks — tasks not yet started by any existing worker. This means tracking claimed tasks in a shared manifest file (not just per-worker configs). The new workers should be launchable as a separate parallel.py invocation that reads the manifest to avoid overlap. (2) Support running multiple model configs simultaneously on the same 500 tasks. E.g. run Qwen+GLM alongside Claude+GPT on SWE-bench Verified, each with their own results dir and workers, sharing the same task list but with separate predictions dirs. This means the run_name should encode the model config (e.g. swebench-verified-qwen-glm vs swebench-verified-opus-gpt). Current state: evals/parallel.py splits tasks into N chunks at launch, writes temp worker configs, spawns subprocesses. Results go to results/{run_name}/worker-{i}/. Predictions go to workspaces-{i}/_swebench_predictions/. Key constraint: must not disrupt the current 5-worker run (PID 56575, generating patches for 500 tasks). The manifest approach should be backwards-compatible — if no manifest exists, fall back to current chunking.

        Batch framing:
        - Execute batch 3 of 4.
        - Actionable task IDs for this batch: ['T7']
        - Already completed task IDs available as dependency context: ['T1', 'T2', 'T3', 'T4', 'T5', 'T6', 'T8', 'T9']

        [
  {
    "id": "T7",
    "description": "Add join mode to evals/parallel.py and CLI. (1) New function join_parallel_run(config_path, run_name, new_workers): load manifest from results/{run_name}/_task_manifest.json (fail with clear error if missing). Load canonical config from results/{run_name}/_run_config.json \u2014 if --config also provided, validate it matches (same benchmark, models) or warn [addresses join-configuration flag]. Print remaining task count via manifest.summary(). Allocate worker IDs via manifest.next_worker_id(). Spawn new_workers subprocesses with temp configs pointing to same manifest. In legacy (non-parallel) mode, the old path is preserved.\n\n**EvalConfig slots=True**: This dataclass uses `slots=True`, which means unknown kwargs to EvalConfig() raise TypeError. This is why T1 must add the new fields to the class \u2014 you can't just pass them through as extra kwargs. But the defaults mean old configs without these fields work fine.\n\n**Don't touch PID 56575**: The running 5-worker job has already-written temp config files on disk. No disruption.\n\n**Testing strategy**: T3 validates the manifest in isolation.         Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

> AGENT

Join mode depends on worker-ID reservation, not just discovery. I’m extending the manifest with explicit reserved worker IDs so a join invocation can allocate under the manifest lock before any new subprocess starts, and I’m also reserving the original `worker-0..worker-N` IDs at run start so a mid-run join cannot collide before those workers have claimed their first task.

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Two goals for the SWE-bench eval harness: (1) Scale to 10 parallel workers mid-run without disrupting existing work. Currently 5 workers each have 100 tasks assigned at launch via contiguous chunking. Need to support adding more workers that pick up UNCLAIMED tasks — tasks not yet started by any existing worker. This means tracking claimed tasks in a shared manifest file (not just per-worker configs). The new workers should be launchable as a separate parallel.py invocation that reads the manifest to avoid overlap. (2) Support running multiple model configs simultaneously on the same 500 tasks. E.g. run Qwen+GLM alongside Claude+GPT on SWE-bench Verified, each with their own results dir and workers, sharing the same task list but with separate predictions dirs. This means the run_name should encode the model config (e.g. swebench-verified-qwen-glm vs swebench-verified-opus-gpt). Current state: evals/parallel.py splits tasks into N chunks at launch, writes temp worker configs, spawns subprocesses. Results go to results/{run_name}/worker-{i}/. Predictions go to workspaces-{i}/_swebench_predictions/. Key constraint: must not disrupt the current 5-worker run (PID 56575, generating patches for 500 tasks). The manifest approach should be backwards-compatible — if no manifest exists, fall back to current chunking.

  "meta_commentary": "**Execution guidance:**\n\n**Parallelism**: T1, T2, and T8 have no dependencies and can be implemented concurrently. T3 and T4 can start as soon as T2 and T1 finish respectively. T5 and T6 are the core integration tasks and depend on both T1 and T2. T7 depends on T5+T6. T9 depends on T8. T10 is the final validation gate.\n\n**Critical path**: T1 \u2192 T5/T6 \u2192 T7 \u2192 T10. The manifest module (T2) is also on the critical path since T5 and T6 both need it.\n\n**Biggest risk**: The orchestrator manifest-wait loop (T6 step 5). The plan leaves the timeout question open \u2014 implement a 4-hour default with a configurable override. On timeout, log which task IDs are stranded in 'claimed' state and proceed to combine/score with whatever is done. Do NOT hang forever.\n\n**Second biggest risk**: The --join config validation (T7). The plan acknowledges this is unresolved. Implement it by saving _run_config.json at run start and having --join compare key fields (benchmark, models, swebench_dataset) against it. Warn loudly on mismatch, but allow override with a --force flag for advanced users.\n\n**Prediction path fix context**: The bug at swe_bench.py:247 is real \u2014 workers write to `workspace_path.parent/_swebench_predictions` while parallel.py:38 reads from `results_root/_swebench_predictions`. These are different directories. The fix in T4 uses config.predictions_dir (set by parallel.py to the results_root path) to align them. In legacy (non-parallel) mode, the old path is preserved.\n\n**EvalConfig slots=True**: This dataclass uses `slots=True`, which means unknown kwargs to EvalConfig() raise TypeError. This is why T1 must add the new fields to the class \u2014 you can't just pass them through as extra kwargs. But the defaults mean old configs without these fields work fine.\n\n**Don't touch PID 56575**: The running 5-worker job has already-written temp config files on disk. Those files don't have the new manifest fields, but since the new fields have defaults, load_config() will construct EvalConfig without them \u2192 manifest_path='' \u2192 legacy mode. No disruption.\n\n**Testing strategy**: T3 validates the manifest in isolation. T10 validates the full integration but needs stubs \u2014 the actual eval loop calls megaplan/hermes which need API keys. Mock at the benchmark.score() boundary to just write a prediction file and return a ScoringResult."
}

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] config-reproducibility: openrouter_params (temperature, top_p) are recorded in config but cannot be applied because neither hermes aiagent nor the megaplan cli expose inference parameter controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] docker-sandbox-init: file_tools.py does not forward docker_mount_cwd_to_workspace when creating the docker sandbox, so if read_file/write_file is the first tool call, the host workspace bind mount may not be set up. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-routing: handler layer hardcodes planned→critique transitions that the plan doesn't explicitly enumerate for update. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] hermes-config: availability check references ~/.hermes/config.yaml for api keys, but hermes stores them in ~/.hermes/.env. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] join-workflow: the --join cli has no enforced source of truth for original run settings, so a mismatched config could attach wrong models/workspace layout to an existing manifest. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] module-layout: hermes_worker.py importing workerresult from workers.py while workers.py imports run_hermes_step from hermes_worker.py creates a circular import. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] orchestrator-lifecycle: the manifest.all_done() polling loop can hang indefinitely if a worker crashes and leaves tasks in 'claimed' state with no timeout or reclaim mechanism. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] research-prompt: plan omits the brief's explicit 'compare against at least 2 search results' requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: proposed framework_versions object schema will be rejected by the strict additionalproperties:false rewriter. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] schema-validation: prompt wording alone cannot prevent empty or content-free consideration entries since the schema only requires severity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] scoring-diffing: git diff head~1 fails when the workspace has only one initial commit before execution begins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] session-lifecycle: _cleanup_hermes_sessions() hard-codes path.home() / '.hermes' / 'sessions', ignoring hermes_home. parallel workers may delete session files from the default location. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] test-coverage: plan references wrong test files for workflow assertions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-collection: session log path is ~/.hermes/sessions/ not ~/.hermes/logs/, and sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so naive reads return cumulative history rather than phase-isolated snapshots. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] trace-instrumentation: the plan captures normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(), not raw http request/response payloads or openrouter response headers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-contract: plan's sample workerresult constructor uses non-existent fields (output, input_tokens, output_tokens) instead of actual fields (payload, raw_output). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: plan's sample function signature (phase, prompt, schema_dir, model) doesn't match the actual worker signature (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] worker-dispatch: research on non-hermes agents silently falls back to stale training data instead of fresh web searches. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] workspace-lifecycle: stale per-worker prediction dirs from crashed prior runs could be globbed into a subsequent run's combined_predictions.jsonl. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

        Note: User explicitly approved this plan at the gate checkpoint.
        Robustness level: standard.

        Requirements:
        - Execute only the actionable tasks in this batch.
        - Treat completed tasks as dependency context, not new work.
        - Return structured JSON only.
        - Only produce `task_updates` for these tasks: [T10]
        - Only produce `sense_check_acknowledgments` for these sense checks: [SC10]
        - Do not include updates for tasks or sense checks outside this batch.
        - Keep `executor_notes` verification-focused.
        - Best-effort progress checkpointing: if `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/.megaplan/plans/two-goals-for-the-swe-bench-20260328-2006/execution_batch_4.json` is writable, checkpoint task and sense-check updates there (not `finalize.json`). The harness owns `finalize.json`.
